Let's formulate our main optimizer with direction options:

So direction d = A - mean(A) (or A - 1). This preserves sum approximately. To reduce max, we need A high for variables involved in active peaks. The mean may shift if boundary weights; subtracting mean ensures no net change. This is safe.

For `n*A - Q`, centering gives A - mean(A) (since subtract Q constant then center: A/n? Let's see d=nA-Q, mean=n mean_A - Q; center -> n(A-mean_A), same as A-mode! So we can use centered A. Nice. The gradient of max for a set of active peaks depends only on the shape of b reversed at those lags. Thus direction is to lower variables that appear as partners in active peaks (weighted), raise variables that don't. This is independent of peak magnitudes if weights sum1. But weights should be chosen to reflect which peaks matter (near max). So top/soft weights crucial.

This suggests a very simple iterative algorithm:
- Choose weights w on high-lag coefficients (those near max).
- Compute A = weighted average of reversed segments corresponding to w.
- Update b by subtracting proportionally to A - mean(A) (additive) or multiplicative.
This is like smoothing: redistribute mass away from positions that frequently pair to form high sums.

We can improve by choosing weights not just top-K but based on all coefficients using an exponential of c, maybe with a "peak cap"? Since objective max, any peak slightly below max can become max after update. We should penalize a band. Use weights w_k = exp((c_k - max(c))/T) for all k, optionally with T small. Then A via FFT. This yields a smooth vector field that may converge to a state where A is constant over support weighted by this softmax. At optimum with many peaks tied, weights spread. This is essentially a game of entropy.

Let's define an update algorithm for an array b of length N, sum=N:
1. c=conv(b,b). Cmax = max(c).
2. Compute weights w:
   - If method topK: select top K indices, weights proportional to (c[idx] - Cmax + Δ)^p? Maybe uniform topK for stability.
   - If softmax all: z=(c-Cmax)/T, T = max(Cmax*temp, (Cmax - second)*0.5). w=exp(z), w/=sum(w). To avoid too much on all (which gives A=1? If w roughly uniform over all lags, A≈mean of b=1, no motion). Need select a subset/band. Use threshold: include c_k > max(0.99 Cmax, Cmax - eps*(Cmax - min)?). But if Cmax much larger than others, should focus max. At near optimum, many peaks close; use all above Cmax - δ. δ adapt: start 0.05*Cmax, increase if stuck.
3. Compute A: If topK small, direct. If all, FFT.
4. d = A - mean(A). Maybe scale by 1/std? Then gradient step:
   - Additive: b = b - alpha * momentum; b=max(0); b*=N/sum(b).
   - Or multiplicative: logb = logb + alpha * d? Wait descent: we want reduce high A, so b_j ∝ exp(-eta d_j) (since d high => decrease). After renorm. Let logb <- logb - eta*d - mean(eta*d) maybe; b=exp(logb); b*=N/sum(b). This preserves positivity and can be combined with AdaGrad.
Which is better? Additive may create zeros but can move large absolute amounts. Multiplicative respects relative changes; if A-mean ~0.1 and b~1, log change small. We can use per-coordinate normalization: alpha = step_size / (np.std(d)+eps) to get comparable log changes. Then logb -= alpha*d_centered. Renorm. This is like projected gradient on log manifold.

Need choose step size. Since d_centered values maybe O(1). Step size 0.1 leads 10% relative changes. We can line search: try several alphas (e.g. 0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3) and pick best score after step? Evaluating FFT for each alpha costly but okay if periodic. But updating with a bad alpha can lose progress; we can keep best and revert. Simpler: use small fixed lr with momentum and occasional line search.

For multiplicative, the objective is invariant to global scaling, so constant component removed. We can include momentum in log space: m = beta*m + (1-beta)*d_centered; logb -= lr*m. Then renorm. Because d_centered mean zero, renorm not huge.

Potential issue: If the current sequence has zeros, log undefined. We can maintain positive lower bound: b = np.maximum(b, 1e-12) before log. Or use additive for zeros.

Let's implement an optimizer function `optimize_sequence(b, start_time, stop_time, method, ...)` that can run many steps and track best. It should use top-level helper functions; no closures/lambda.

Pseudo:
```
def calc_scores(b):
    c = conv_square(b)
    C = c.max()
    S = b.sum()
    return 2*C/(S*S/N? actually score=2n*C/(S^2) if n=len; for b sum n score=2C/n)
```
For internal, if b normalized sum=n, score_fast=2*C/n. For score with any sum, use 2*n*C/(S*S).

`active_vector_weights(c, mode, topK, temp, threshold)`:
- c length L=2n-1. Cmax=c.max()
- If method 'top_partition': Use np.argpartition(c, -topK)[-topK:] to get top indices. weights = np.ones(topK)/topK or proportional to (c[top]- (Cmax - band))? Use uniform top for stability. - If 'all_softmax': z=(c-Cmax)/T, T = max(Cmax*temp, (Cmax - np.partition(c, L-K)*?)). w=exp(z); sum; if sum too large? Could be okay. We may restrict to threshold to avoid the uniform all issue. Use z for selected c above threshold.

Let's design a robust single optimizer run with annealing:
- Initialize b positive.
- Parameters: topK from schedule (16 -> 256), beta=0.9, lr=0.05 maybe, noise=0.01.
- For each step until time:
   - compute c, Cmax, score.
   - if score < best: update best, reset stuck.
   - compute weights (topK or band).
   - compute A (direct for K<100 else FFT). For direct, weights vector w and idx. If K small, loop over idx. For FFT, compute full A.
   - d = A - A.mean()
   - if d.std small -> add noise/restart.
   - m = beta*m + (1-beta)*d
   - lr decays slowly; maybe scale = lr / (np.std(m)+eps) to control relative step.
   - logb += -scale*m? Wait m may grow; scale = lr / (np.std(m)+eps) gives step O(lr). If m std large, alpha smaller. But if d std small, we want larger? Use adaptive: step_size = lr * (b_mean / (np.std(m)+eps))? b_mean=1. Then alpha=step_size? logb -= alpha*m. If m std ~1, alpha=lr.
   - logb -= mean(logb) maybe; b=exp(logb); b*=N/sum(b); b=np.maximum(b, eps).
   - To prevent collapse to few large values, maybe apply a "regularization" towards current best or uniform: logb = (1-regular) logb + regular * log(best or uniform)? But can ruin. We can mix at restarts.
   - Periodically add random noise in log-space: logb += sigma*randn; sigma annealed.
   - Occasionally (e.g. every 5000 steps or if stuck) try a local line search or re-normalize to best + noise.

But we need be careful: log-multiplicative with d=A-mean can have a fixed point where A constant. But if A constant=1, all good. It may converge to a uniform target? The weights are updated by c, so A constant means weighted convolution contributions flat. This is plausible.

Update:
```
p = weights(c) # length L, sum=1
A = weighted partner vector (∑ p_k b_{k-j})
q = ∑ p_k c_k / S   # S=sum b

normalize h (equiv b/=mean)
```

```
import numpy as np, time, scipy.fft as spfft (ifft fft)
# no evaluate_sequence import

def _fft_conv_sq(a): n=len(a); out_len=2n-1; N=1<<(out_len-1).bit_length(); F=spfft.rfft(a,N); C=spfft.irfft(F*F,N)[:out_len]; C=np.maximum(C,0) # due to numerical negative? For nonnegative maybe small neg; max safe.
   return C
```

`_score_from_c(c,n): C=max; return 2*n*C/(sum?)` But if normalized sum n, score=2*C/n. To be exact, use evaluate fast with sum: `2*n*C/(S*S)`. We can maintain sum n exactly.

`_normalize_sum(x, S): x=np.maximum(x,0); s=x.sum(); if s==0 uniform; return x*(S/s)`.

`_compute_A_direct(b, idx, weights)`:
```
n=len(b); A=np.zeros(n)
for w, k in zip(weights, idx):
   lo=max(0, k-n+1); hi=min(n-1, k)
   length=hi-lo+1
   # b segment from k-hi to k-lo reversed
   seg=b[k-hi:k-lo+1][::-1]
   A[lo:hi+1] += w*seg
return A
```
Check: k-hi≥0? hi=min(n-1,k) => k-hi≥0. k-lo≤n-1? lo=max(0,k-n+1) => k-lo≤n-1. good.

`_compute_A_fft(b, p)`:
L=2n-1. w_rev = p[::-1]
Nf=1<<(L-1).bit_length()
Fb=spfft.rfft(b,Nf); Fw=spfft.rfft(w_rev,Nf); conv=spfft.irfft(Fb*Fw,Nf)[:L]; A = conv[L-1 - np.arange(n)]? Earlier d/2? We need A_j=∑ p_k b_{k-j}. Conv of b and w_rev length L: conv[m]=∑_r b_r w_rev[m-r]. w_rev[t]=p[L-1-t]. conv[m]=∑_r b_r p[L-1-(m-r)] =∑_r b_r p[L-1-m+r]. We want index j: ∑_r b_r p[j+r]. Set L-1-m = j => m=L-1-j. So A[j]=conv[L-1-j]. Good.
Use real. Since p sum1, A dimension b.

But if p sparse topK, direct faster and avoids FFT overhead. For K<=128 use direct, else FFT.

`_make_weights(c, mode, K, temp, threshold, rng)`:

   T = max(1e-12, temp * Cmax). If vals are close, maybe all equal.

- 'soft_all': T = max(Cmax*temp, 1e-3); z=(c-Cmax)/T; w=exp(z); normalize. To prevent uniform all, if sum w too large relative L? Hard. Use top band maybe better.

Weighted peak q = dot(weights, c[idx]). For soft all, q=dot(p,c).

`_nm_step(b, w, idx/q, lr, momentum, beta, mode, step_clip)`:
- S = b.sum() (should n)
- A=...
- q = Q/S
- step = b*(A - q)
- center? Since ∑step should 0 if A and q exact with full p and direct (boundary maybe slight). To guard numerical and boundary effects, we can subtract step.mean() to keep zero sum? The gradient on h for softmax has ∑step=0 only if p sum1 and A uses all j. For boundary p, ∑A maybe less than S, so ∑step = Q - q S? But q=Q/S, so ∑step=Q - Q=0 algebraically? Let's check ∑_j step_j = ∑ b_j A_j - q∑b_j = Q - Q=0. This holds regardless of boundary because ∑_j A_j over j? We need ∑_j b_j A_j=Q. A_j=∑_k p_k b_{k-j}; ∑_j b_j A_j =∑_k p_k ∑_j b_j b_{k-j}=∑p c=Q. Yes holds. So step sum zero exactly (modulo numerical). Let's elaborate NM optimizer implementation to be efficient and robust.

Top-level parameters for a run:
```
def nm_search(b0, deadline, name, rng, init_lr=0.05, beta=0.9, temp=0.02, K_init=16, K_end=128, top_mode='soft', use_adam=False, noise=0.0, eval_interval=10):
   n=len(b0); b=normalize(b0,n); best=b.copy(); best_score=fast_score(b)
   log? no
   mom=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n)
   lr=init_lr; stuck=0; step=0; K=K_init
   while time < deadline:
      c=conv_sq(b); C=c.max(); sc=2*C/n (or exact if sum not n)
      if sc < best_score -1e-10: best=b.copy(); best_score=sc; stuck=0
      else: stuck +=1
      # adapt K maybe based on score progress? K = min(K_end, K_init + step//1000)
      idx,w = get_weights(c, ...); A=...
      Q=np.dot(w,c[idx]) if idx else...
      step_vec = b * (A - Q/n)
      step_vec -= step_vec.mean()  # numeric
      # line scale: norm = np.linalg.norm(step_vec)/(np.sqrt(n)) maybe
      lr_eff = lr / (np.sqrt(np.mean(step_vec*step_vec)) + 1e-12) ? If step std ~1, lr_eff=lr.
      # momentum/adam
      mom = beta*mom + (1-beta)*step_vec
      v = beta2*v + (1-beta2)*step_vec*step_vec
      delta = lr_eff * mom / (np.sqrt(v)+1e-6)  # if use_adam else lr_eff*mom
      delta = np.clip(delta, -max_log, max_log)
      delta -= delta.mean()
      b = b * np.exp(-delta)
      b = np.maximum(b, 1e-12)
      b *= n/b.sum()
      # maybe occasionally line search? skip
      step +=1
      # if stuck > some: noise/mix/restart
   return best,best_score
```

The step vector dimension: A and b are O(1) if sum n. Q/n is O(1). step O(1). Adam normalizes, so lr is relative (Adam lr typical 0.05). Momentum beta=0.9. Good.

But if step_vec is near zero (stagnation), Adam denominator tiny, delta maybe 0. Add noise.

Need choose K/temp. For NM, weights p should be on upper tail. Let's define `get_weights_top_soft(c, K, temp_frac, rng)`:
```
L=c.size; C=c.max()
if K>=L: idx=np.arange(L); vals=c
else: idx=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
vals=c[idx]
# T based on spread in top values
span = C - vals.min()
T = max(1e-9, max(temp_frac*C, 0.1*span))
z=(vals-C)/T
w=np.exp(z); w/=w.sum()
```
If K small and top values all equal, z=0, uniform. If span small, T small (temp_frac*C), strongly favor absolute highest. But if hard max, top1 would be selected; K>1 with z small gives near uniform among top close. For stagnation, increase K to spread weights, lower temp? Actually if stuck due to chattering single peak, increasing K helps. If stuck due to flat direction, decreasing K? We can schedule K from small to large. temp_frac maybe 0.01.

Alternative 'band' weights using threshold based on C - 0.2*C. If C~n, threshold 0.8C; includes coefficients within 20% of max. At optimum target, many top coefficients maybe within few percent. Could include too many -> weak. Use topK select.

Let's test NM on uniform: c triangular. Top K includes center; A_j for center 1 interior, endpoints less if K not center? For k=n-1, A_j=1 all j (since all b=1). q=C/S=n/n=1. step=0. If K includes k=n-2, A_j=1 for j<=n-2, 0 for j=n-1? q=(n-1)/n. step at j=n-1: b*(0 -0.99)= -0.99 -> h increases last variable (because negative delta? step negative -> exp(-lr*m) increases). Good breaks uniform by increasing endpoint. So soft K>1 needed. We should ensure initial uniform (or parent) gets perturbed. We can add small random noise init.

What about values b not O(1) but some large (O(√n))? Then step may be large for large b. Fortunate if large b is in high A, it reduces; if large b in low A, it may increase? Could cause blow-up. To prevent, use `np.clip(step, -max_abs, max_abs)` where max_abs maybe 5 or 10. If step large, clip. Since step should be O(1) for typical values; large b (10) may step up to 10. Clip to 2? If b=10 and A=2, q=0.75, step=10*1.25=12.5, clip to2, moderate. Good. Use max_step=3 maybe. After highest normalization, sum stable.

Momentum/Adam with clipped step keeps changes bounded. lr typical 0.1; log changes ≤0.3 per step. Good.

```

Let's design final `propose_candidate` detailed:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=123, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    end = time.monotonic() + max(10, budget_s - 20)
    # Load parent
    parent=None
    try: parent = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except: parent=None
    if parent is not None and parent.size>100:
       parent = np.clip(parent,0,1000); n_parent=parent.size
       parent = normalize(parent, n_parent)
       best=parent.copy(); best_score=evaluate_sequence(best.tolist())
    else:
       n_parent=5582; parent=uniform(n_parent); best=parent; best_score=evaluate...
    # maybe evaluate exact parent; if not finite, inf.
    # quick warmup parent with nm_search short (e.g. 120s or until end-300)
    # But nm_search returns best and score; update.
    # Prepare inits for same n:
       exp r: for r in [0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0]: x=np.linspace(0,1,n); b=np.exp(r*x); maybe also exp(-r*x) (reversal same if optimizer deterministic? include if different random noise)
       ramp: 1+1.5*x
       sin: 1+0.9*np.sin(2*pi*x+phase) clipped positive
       noise: best* exp(0.2 randn)
       parent smoothed? np.convolve with small kernel? maybe.
    # Quick trials: allocate e.g. remaining*0.15 divided among up to 5 inits, each min 20s. After each, evaluate exact if internal improved, update best.
    # Long trial from best: remaining*0.6 with robust config.
    # Trial length increase: if parent not None and n_parent < 7000 and time left > 400: interpolate best to n2 = min(8000, int(n_parent*1.25)); run 20% budget.
    # Final polish best with small lr for 60s.
    # Evaluate final best exact; return list.
```

Need be careful not to exceed end. Use helper `time_left()`.

But what if parent score already exact 1.517; our quick trials may not improve and waste time; long parent should. Need hyperparameters robust enough. Let's think about learning rates. If Adam normalizes step, lr=0.1 may be good. We can do a mini line search in code: at start of a run, try lr values [0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2] for a few steps? But time. We can use adaptive: start lr=0.05; if no improvement after 1000 steps, increase? Actually if too small, no improvement; too large, worse. We can monitor `best_score` and `current_score`. If current score > best by >0.01 for many steps, reduce. If current score close and not improving, maybe increase. Simpler: fix lr=0.1 with momentum; Adam robust.

Let's visualize update: b_i *= exp(-delta_i). If delta ~ lr*m/sqrt(v). Adam normalizes each coordinate, so effective relative change per step ~lr. lr=0.1 gives 10% changes. This could be too aggressive for a smooth optimization; but with momentum and best tracking, maybe okay. lr=0.05 safer. We'll use lr=0.05 for full runs, 0.1 for short? We can try both.

Maybe an even better schedule: Use annealed lr: high (0.2) early to explore, low (0.02) late. For each run: lr = lr_end + (lr_start-lr_end)*max(0,1 - step/steps_est). We don't know steps; use time fraction.

Run config example:
```
def run_nm(b, t_end, seed, lr_start=0.1, lr_end=0.01, K_start=16, K_end=128, temp_frac=0.02, beta=0.9, max_step=2.0, noise_prob=0.01, name=''):
   start_time=time.monotonic()
   b=normalize copy; best=...
   mom/v zeros
   K=K_start
   while time_left:
      frac = (time.monotonic()-start_time)/max(t_end-start_time,1)
      lr = lr_end + (lr_start-lr_end)*(1-frac)
      K = int(K_start + (K_end-K_start)*frac)
      c=conv_sq(b); ...
      idx, weights = top_soft(c,K,temp_frac)
      A=...
      step = b*(A - Q/n)
      step -= step.mean(); step = np.clip(step, -max_step, max_step)
      # If step norm small, add random? 
      mom=...; v=...
      delta = lr * mom / (np.sqrt(v)+1e-8)
      delta = np.clip(delta, -2.0, 2.0); delta -= delta.mean()
      b *= np.exp(-delta)
      b = np.maximum(b, 1e-12); b*=n/b.sum()
      # occasional noise: if rng.random()<noise_prob and stuck... 
   return best,best_score
```

_SMOOTH_N = 0
_SMOOTH_T = 1.0
_SMOOTH_LAST_SCORE = None

def _softmax_b(h):
    n=_SMOOTH_N
    hm=h - np.max(h)
    e=np.exp(hm)
    s=e.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: e=np.ones(n)/n
    return n*e/s

def _smooth_val(h):
   b=_softmax_b(h); c=_conv_square(b); if size... z=(c-cmax)/T; return cmax + T*(np.log(np.sum(np.exp(z))))

def _smooth_grad(h):

```

For FFT A, p full length L:
```
def _A_fft(b,p):
    n=len(b); L=2*n-1; Nf=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
    w_rev = p[::-1]
    fb = spfft.rfft(b, Nf); fp = spfft.rfft(w_rev, Nf)
    conv = spfft.irfft(fb*fp, Nf)[:L]
    A = conv[L-1 - np.arange(n)]
    return A
```

```
import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft
from scipy.optimize import minimize

_SMOOTH_N = 0
_SMOOTH_T = 1.0
_SMOOTH_CMAX = 0.0
_SMOOTH_ZSUM = 1.0

def _conv_square(a):
    n = int(np.size(a))
    L = 2*n - 1
    N = 1 << (L - 1).bit_length()
    fa = rfft(a, N)
    fc = irfft(fa * fa, N)
    fc = fc[:L]
    # remove tiny negative?
    # fc = np.where(fc < 0, 0.0, fc) might cost; but max okay
    return fc

def _fast_score_from_c(c, n, S):
    C = float(np.max(c))
    if C <= 0: return 0.0? if S small inf
    if S < 1e-12: return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * C / (S*S))
```
For normalized sum n, score=2*C/n. `_fast_score(b)` compute c and S.

Normalize:
```
def _normalize(a, S):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = np.clip(a, -np.inf, 1000.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    # if sum zero -> uniform
    s = a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-30:
        return np.full(a.shape, S / a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    a = a * (S / s)
    # clip again after scaling
    a = np.minimum(a, 1000.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
    # renormalize after clip? If clip active, sum not S.
    s2=a.sum()
    if s2>0: a = a * (S/s2)
    a = np.clip(a, 1e-12, 1000.0)
    return a
```
If S/n >1000? impossible (S=n ->1). Good.

Top indices:
```
def _top_soft_weights(c, K, temp_frac):
    L=c.size; C=c.max()
    if K >= L:
       idx=np.arange(L); vals=c
    else:
       idx=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
       vals=c[idx]
    span = C - float(np.min(vals))
    T = max(1e-9, max(temp_frac*C, 0.1*span))  # maybe span term? If span large, broad; if small, C*temp.
    z = (vals - C) / T
    w = np.exp(z)
    ws = w.sum()
    if ws <=0 or not finite: w = np.ones_like(w)/K
    else: w /= ws
    return idx, w, C*? return C, Q.dot
```

Fine. Use T=temp_frac*C. If C=4000, T=80. If top values span 10, weights vary e^{-10/80}=0.88, broad; okay. If span 500, weights vary e^{-6}=0.002, focus. Good. So T=temp_frac*C. No need span. def _nm_search(b0, t_end, seed, name='', lr_start=0.08, lr_end=0.01, K_start=16, K_end=192, temp_frac=0.02, beta=0.9, beta2=0.999, max_step=3.0, noise_sigma=0.15, direct_max=128):
```

Inside, `total = max(1.0, float(t_end - time.monotonic()))`.
Loop:
```
step_count=0; no_improve=0; start_t=time.monotonic()
b = _normalize(b0, n)
best = b.copy(); best_score = _fast_score(b) # compute c? We'll compute first inside; maybe initialize with 2*maxconv/n.
mom=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n)
while time.monotonic() < t_end:
    c = _conv_square(b)
    C = float(np.max(c)); S=float(b.sum()); sc = 2.0*n*C/(S*S)
    if not np.isfinite(sc): break
    if sc < best_score - 1e-10:
        best_score = sc; best = b.copy(); no_improve=0; last_improve=time.monotonic()
    else:
        no_improve += 1
    elapsed = time.monotonic() - start_t
    frac = min(1.0, elapsed / total)
    K = int(K_start + (K_end-K_start)*frac)
    if K < 4: K=4
    if K > 2n-2: K=2n-2
    idx,w,Cdot? = _top_soft_weights(c,K,temp_frac)
    Q = float(np.dot(w, c[idx]))
    if K <= direct_max:
       A = _A_direct(b, idx, w)
    else:
       p=np.zeros(2*n-1); p[idx]=w; A=_A_fft(b,p)
    q = Q / S
    grad = b * (A - q)
    grad -= grad.mean()
    if not finite: break
    gnorm = np.linalg.norm(grad)
    if gnorm < 1e-12:
       # add noise
       b = _add_noise(b, sigma=noise_sigma, rng); mom*=0; v*=0; no_improve=0
       continue
    # adaptive lr
    alpha = lr_end + (lr_start-lr_end)*(1.0-frac)
    # if no_improve large, reduce alpha
    if no_improve > 5000:
       alpha *= max(0.1, 1.0 - 0.1*(no_improve//5000))
    mom = beta*mom + (1-beta)*grad
    v = beta2*v + (1-beta2)*grad*grad
    delta = alpha * mom / (np.sqrt(v) + 1e-8)
    delta = np.clip(delta, -max_step, max_step)
    delta -= delta.mean()
    b = b * np.exp(-delta)
    b = np.clip(b, 1e-12, 1000.0)
    b *= n / b.sum()
    # Optional: if score increased a lot (not computed until next), we can't know.
    step_count +=1
```

```
def _lbfgs_refine(b, max_iter=80, T=10.0):
    global _SMOOTH_N, _SMOOTH_T
    n=len(b); b=_normalize(b,n); _SMOOTH_N=n; _SMOOTH_T=T
    h0=np.log(np.maximum(b,1e-300) / n)
    try:
       res=minimize(_smooth_value, h0, jac=_smooth_grad, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxiter': max_iter, 'ftol':1e-9, 'gtol':1e-7})
    except Exception:
       return b, _fast_score(b)
    h=res.x if res.success? but may not; if finite:
       b2 = n*np.exp(h-h.max()); b2=sum normalize
       sc2=_fast_score(b2)
       if finite and sc2 < _fast_score(b): return b2, sc2
    return b, _fast_score(b)
```

However, `minimize` calls objective and gradient many times, and each gradient uses FFT p dense; T set. There is a possibility that L-BFGS takes a step causing h with large spread, softmax underflow for many variables -> b zeros, gradient zero, stalls. To prevent, we can add a small lower bound by adding regularization in objective: `val += 1e-6 * n * (h - h.mean())@(h - h.mean())`? This biases toward equal (uniform) slightly. Detailed `_normalize`:
```
def _normalize(a, S):
    a = np.array(a, dtype=np.float64, copy=True)
    if a.size == 0: return a
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    a = np.minimum(a, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-30 or not np.isfinite(s):
        a.fill(S / a.size)
        return a
    a *= S / s
    a = np.clip(a, 1e-12, 1000.0)
    s2 = float(np.sum(a))
    if s2 > 0:
       a *= S / s2
    a = np.clip(a, 1e-12, 1000.0)
    return a
```
If a.size > S/1e-12? no.

`_fast_score` using conv:
```
def _fast_score(b):
    n=len(b); c=_conv_square(b); C=np.max(c); S=sum(b); if S<0.01? For internal no. return 2*n*C/(S*S)
```
For b normalized n, exact fast. If c has negative due roundoff, max positive.

```
def _top_soft_weights(c, K, temp_frac):
   L=c.size; C=float(np.max(c)); K=min(K,L)
   if K <=0: ...
   if K == L:
      idx=np.arange(L); vals=c
   else:
      kth=L-K
      idx=np.argpartition(c, kth)[L-K:]
      vals=c[idx]
   T=max(1e-9, temp_frac*C)
   z=(vals-C)/T
   # prevent overflow: z<=0
   w=np.exp(z)
   ws=w.sum()
   if ws<=0 or not np.isfinite(ws):
      w=np.ones_like(vals)/K
   else: w=w/ws
   return idx,w
```
If temp_frac too small (0), T=1e-9, z underflows except max; weights one-hot. We'll use 0.02.

def _perturb_sequence(b, seed_or_rng, sigma):
    n=len(b); rng=...
    logb=np.log(np.maximum(b,1e-12))
    logb += rng.standard_normal(n)*sigma
    return _normalize(np.exp(logb - logb.max()), n)
```

def _evaluate_candidate(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size==0: return np.inf
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return np.inf
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    if a.sum() < 0.01: return np.inf
    try: sc=float(evaluate_sequence(a.tolist()))
    except Exception: return np.inf
    return sc if np.isfinite(sc) else np.inf

Let's write `_nm_search` fully:
```
def _nm_search(b0, t_end, seed, name='', lr_start=0.08, lr_end=0.015, K_start=16, K_end=192, temp_frac=0.01, beta=0.9, max_step=1.6, noise_sigma=0.2, direct_max=128):
    n = int(np.size(b0))
    if n <= 0:
       return b0, np.inf
    b = _normalize(b0, float(n))
    c0 = _conv_square(b); best_score = 2.0*n*float(np.max(c0))/ (float(b.sum())**2)
    best = b.copy()
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    mom = np.zeros(n)
    step_count=0; no_improve=0; cur_temp=temp_frac
    start_time=time.monotonic()
    total_time=max(1.0, float(t_end-time.monotonic()))
    while time.monotonic() < t_end:
       c = _conv_square(b)
       C = float(np.max(c)); S=float(np.sum(b)); sc=2.0*n*C/(S*S)
       if not np.isfinite(sc): break
       improved = False
       if sc < best_score - 1e-10:
          best_score = sc; best=b.copy(); improved=True
       if improved:
          no_improve=0; cur_temp=temp_frac
       else:
          no_improve += 1
          if no_improve > 20000:
             b = _perturb_sequence(best, rng, noise_sigma)
             mom = np.zeros(n)
             no_improve=0; cur_temp=temp_frac
             continue
          elif no_improve > 6000:
             cur_temp = min(0.08, cur_temp*1.05 + 1e-5)
       elapsed = time.monotonic()-start_time
       frac = min(1.0, elapsed/total_time)
       K = int(K_start + (K_end-K_start)*frac)
       if K < 4: K=4
       L=2*n-1
       if K > L: K=L
       idx,w = _top_soft_weights(c, K, cur_temp)
       A = _A_direct(b, idx, w) if K <= direct_max else _A_fft(b, np.zeros(L)??? )
```
For FFT branch: need p full.
```
       if K <= direct_max:
           A = _A_direct(...)
       else:
           p=np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64); p[idx]=w; A=_A_fft(b,p)
       Q = float(np.dot(w, c[idx]))
       q = Q / S
       grad = b * (A - q)
       grad -= grad.mean()
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)): break
       gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
       if gnorm < 1e-12:
           b = _perturb_sequence(best, rng, noise_sigma*0.5)
           mom = np.zeros(n); no_improve=0; continue
       mom = beta*mom + (1.0-beta)*grad
       mnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(mom))
       if mnorm < 1e-12: ...
       alpha = lr_end + (lr_start-lr_end)*(1.0-frac)
       delta = alpha * (mom / mnorm)
       # optionally scale by gnorm? If gnorm is very large, maybe delta too constant; if gnorm very small, still unit. Could use scale = min(1.0, max(0.05, gnorm/ (np.sqrt(n)*?)))
```
Using norm not RMS. `mnorm` could be ~norm, delta components alpha*mom/norm -> RMS alpha/√n, too small! I earlier wanted RMS. If `delta = alpha * mom / mnorm`, vector has norm alpha, components std alpha/√n. For n=5582, alpha=0.05, std per coordinate 0.00067, too small. We want components O(alpha). So use RMS normalization: `denom = float(np.linalg.norm(mom) / np.sqrt(n))`; `delta = alpha * mom / denom`. Then mom/denom has norm √n, RMS1. Good. Similarly gnorm maybe RMS. We can check gradient norm in RMS: `gnorm = norm(grad)/sqrt(n)`. For sticking, if RMS tiny.

So:
```
gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(grad)) / np.sqrt(n)
...
mnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(mom)) / np.sqrt(n)
delta = alpha * (mom / mnorm)
```
Then delta RMS=alpha. Good. Clip.

If gradient has some large coordinates, RMS maybe O(1). Good.

Could use `delta = alpha * (mom / (mnorm + 1e-12))`. If mnorm zero, skip.

`grad` values may be O(10), mnorm O(10), delta RMS alpha. Good.

Let's write `_maybe_update` top-level:
```
def _maybe_update(best_seq, best_score, candidate):
    n = int(np.size(candidate))
    if n <=0: return best_seq,best_score
    cand = _normalize(candidate, float(n))
    sc = _evaluate_candidate(cand)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
       return cand, sc
    return best_seq,best_score
```

def _smooth_value(h):
    n=_SMOOTH_N
    if n<=0: return 0.0
    T=_SMOOTH_T
    hm = h - float(np.max(h))
    e = np.exp(hm)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        b = np.full(n, 1.0)
    else:
        b = n * e / s
    c = _conv_square(b)
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    z = (c - cmax) / T
    ez = np.exp(z)
    s2 = float(np.sum(ez))
    if s2 <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s2):
        return cmax
    return cmax + T * np.log(s2)

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative step-function sequence minimizing
2*n*max(conv(a,a))/(sum(a)**2).

Main ideas:
- Self-convolution is computed by FFT for fast internal evaluation.
- Use a multiplicative natural-gradient / Frank-Wolfe-style update:
  choose weights on the largest convolution peaks, form the weighted
  partner vector A_j = sum_k w_k a_{k-j}, and update
      log(a_j) -= eta * normalized(a_j * (A_j - Q/S))
  which moves mass away from variables that build active peaks.
- Several initializations (parent, exponential/ramp, coarse-global search)
  and annealed hyperparameters are used to avoid local minima.
- Optional L-BFGS smoothing on a softmax parameterization polishes candidates.
"""

import time
import numpy as np

try:
    from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft
except Exception:
    from numpy.fft import rfft, irfft

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize
except Exception:
    minimize = None

# Globals for L-BFGS helper functions (top-level, no closures).
_SMOOTH_N = 0
_SMOOTH_T = 1.0


def _conv_square(a):
    """Linear convolution a*a using FFT."""
    n = int(np.size(a))
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    N = 1 << (L - 1).bit_length()
    fa = rfft(a, N)
    fc = irfft(fa * fa, N)
    return fc[:L].astype(np.float64, copy=False)


def _normalize(a, S):
    """Make a real nonnegative array of sum S, respecting [0, 1000]."""
    a = np.array(a, dtype=np.float64, copy=True)
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    a = np.minimum(a, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-30:
        a.fill(S / max(1, a.size))
        return a
    a *= S / s
    a = np.clip(a, 1e-12, 1000.0)
    s2 = float(np.sum(a))
    if s2 > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s2):
        a *= S / s2
    a = np.clip(a, 1e-12, 1000.0)
    return a


def _fast_score(b):
    """Fast internal score using FFT."""
    n = int(np.size(b))
    if n <= 0:
        return np.inf
    c = _conv_square(b)
    C = float(np.max(c))
    S = float(np.sum(b))
    if S < 1e-9:
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * C / (S * S))


def _interpolate(a, n_out):
    """Interpolate a positive array to a new length."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m = a.size
    if m == 0 or n_out <= 0:
        return np.ones(max(1, int(n_out)))
    if m == int(n_out):
        return a.copy()
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, int(n_out))
    return np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)


def _top_soft_weights(c, K, temp_frac):
    """
    Choose weights on the K largest convolution coefficients.
    If temp_frac <= 0, use uniform weights on those K.
    """
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    L = c.size
    K = int(max(1, min(K, L)))
    C = float(np.max(c))

    if K >= L:
        idx = np.arange(L, dtype=np.int64)
        vals = c
    else:
        kth = L - K
        idx = np.argpartition(c, kth)[L - K:]
        vals = c[idx]

    if temp_frac <= 0.0:
        w = np.full(K, 1.0 / K, dtype=np.float64)
        return idx, w

    T = max(1e-9, temp_frac * max(1.0, C))
    z = (vals - C) / T
    w = np.exp(z)
    ws = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
        w = np.full(K, 1.0 / K, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        w = w / ws
    return idx, w


def _A_direct(b, idx, w):
    """
    A_j = sum_k w_k b_{k-j}
    for a small set of active lags k.
    """
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    n = b.size
    A = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for t in range(len(idx)):
        k = int(idx[t])
        wt = float(w[t])
        if wt == 0.0:
            continue
        lo = k - n + 1
        if lo < 0:
            lo = 0
        hi = k
        if hi >= n:
            hi = n - 1
        if hi < lo:
            continue
        # Need b[k-j] for j=lo..hi, which is b[k-hi : k-lo+1] reversed.
        A[lo:hi + 1] += wt * b[k - hi:k - lo + 1][::-1]
    return A


def _A_fft(b, p):
    """
    A_j = sum_k p_k b_{k-j}, for a dense weight vector p of length 2n-1.
    """
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    n = b.size
    L = 2 * n - 1
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size != L:
        p = np.resize(p, L)
    N = 1 << (L - 1).bit_length()
    pb = rfft(b, N)
    pp = rfft(p[::-1], N)
    conv = irfft(pb * pp, N)[:L]
    out_idx = L - 1 - np.arange(n)
    return conv[out_idx].astype(np.float64, copy=False)


def _perturb_sequence(b, rng, sigma):
    """Multiplicative log-noise around a nonnegative sequence."""
    n = b.size
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    logb = np.log(np.maximum(b, 1e-12))
    logb = logb - float(np.mean(logb))
    logb += rng.standard_normal(n) * float(sigma)
    out = np.exp(logb - float(np.max(logb)))
    return _normalize(out, float(n))


def _nm_search(b0, t_end, seed,
               lr_start=0.08, lr_end=0.012,
               K_start=16, K_end=220,
               temp_frac=0.01, beta=0.90,
               max_step=1.8, noise_sigma=0.20,
               direct_max=160):
    """
    Multiplicative natural-gradient search with annealed K/temperature/step.
    Returns (best_array, best_internal_score).
    """
    b0 = np.asarray(b0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = b0.size
    if n <= 0:
        return b0, np.inf

    b = _normalize(b0, float(n))
    best = b.copy()
    best_score = _fast_score(b)
    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
        return best, np.inf

    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    mom = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    start_t = time.monotonic()
    total_t = max(1.0, float(t_end - start_t))
    cur_temp = float(temp_frac)
    no_improve = 0
    step_count = 0

    while time.monotonic() < t_end:
        c = _conv_square(b)
        if c.size == 0:
            break
        Cmax = float(np.max(c))
        S = float(np.sum(b))
        if S <= 1e-12 or not np.isfinite(S):
            break
        sc = 2.0 * n * Cmax / (S * S)
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            break

        if sc < best_score - 1e-10:
            best_score = sc
            best = b.copy()
            no_improve = 0
            cur_temp = float(temp_frac)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 25000:
                b = _perturb_sequence(best, rng, noise_sigma)
                mom = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                no_improve = 0
                cur_temp = float(temp_frac)
                continue
            elif no_improve > 8000:
                cur_temp = min(0.08, cur_temp * 1.05 + 1e-5)

        elapsed = max(1e-9, time.monotonic() - start_t)
        frac = min(1.0, elapsed / total_t)

        K = int(K_start + (K_end - K_start) * frac)
        L = 2 * n - 1
        K = max(4, min(K, L))

        idx, w = _top_soft_weights(c, K, cur_temp)

        if K <= direct_max:
            A = _A_direct(b, idx, w)
        else:
            p = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
            p[idx] = w
            A = _A_fft(b, p)

        Q = float(np.dot(w, c[idx]))
        q = Q / S

        # Direction in log-space: natural gradient for a peaked-convolution objective.
        grad = b * (A - q)
        grad -= float(np.mean(grad))

        if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
            break

        gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(grad)) / np.sqrt(n)
        if gnorm < 1e-12:
            b = _perturb_sequence(best, rng, 0.5 * noise_sigma)
            mom = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            no_improve = 0
            continue

        mom = beta * mom + (1.0 - beta) * grad
        mnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(mom)) / np.sqrt(n)
        if mnorm < 1e-12:
            continue

        alpha = lr_end + (lr_start - lr_end) * (1.0 - frac)
        delta = (alpha / mnorm) * mom
        delta = np.clip(delta, -max_step, max_step)
        delta -= float(np.mean(delta))

        b = b * np.exp(-delta)
        b = np.clip(b, 1e-12, 1000.0)
        bs = float(np.sum(b))
        if bs > 0.0 and np.isfinite(bs):
            b *= n / bs
        else:
            b = best.copy()

        step_count += 1

    return best, best_score


def _smooth_b_from_h(h):
    """b = n * softmax(h)."""
    n = int(_SMOOTH_N)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    hm = h - float(np.max(h))
    e = np.exp(hm)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    return n * (e / s)


def _smooth_value(h):
    """Smooth max-convolution objective H_T(h)=T log sum exp(c_k/T)."""
    n = int(_SMOOTH_N)
    T = float(_SMOOTH_T)
    if n <= 0 or T <= 0.0:
        return 0.0
    b = _smooth_b_from_h(h)
    c = _conv_square(b)
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    z = (c - cmax) / T
    ez = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(ez))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return cmax
    return float(cmax + T * np.log(s))


def _smooth_grad(h):
    """Gradient of H_T wrt h for b=n*softmax(h)."""
    n = int(_SMOOTH_N)
    T = float(_SMOOTH_T)
    if n <= 0 or T <= 0.0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    b = _smooth_b_from_h(h)
    c = _conv_square(b)
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    z = (c - cmax) / T
    p = np.exp(z)
    ps = float(np.sum(p))
    if ps <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(ps):
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    p = p / ps

    A = _A_fft(b, p)
    S = float(np.sum(b))
    Q = float(np.dot(p, c))
    grad = b * (A - Q / S)
    grad -= float(np.mean(grad))
    return 2.0 * grad


def _lbfgs_refine(b, max_iter=30, T=10.0):
    """
    Optional L-BFGS polish on h variables with b=n*softmax(h).
    Returns (candidate, internal_score).
    """
    if minimize is None:
        sc = _fast_score(b)
        return b, sc

    n = int(np.size(b))
    if n <= 0:
        return b, np.inf

    b = _normalize(b, float(n))
    base_score = _fast_score(b)

    global _SMOOTH_N, _SMOOTH_T
    _SMOOTH_N = n
    _SMOOTH_T = max(1.0, float(T))

    h0 = np.log(np.maximum(b, 1e-300) / float(n))
    h0 -= float(np.mean(h0))

    try:
        res = minimize(
            _smooth_value,
            h0,
            jac=_smooth_grad,
            method="L-BFGS-B",
            options={"maxiter": int(max_iter), "ftol": 1e-9, "gtol": 1e-7, "maxcor": 8}
        )
    except Exception:
        return b, base_score

    if hasattr(res, "x") and res.x is not None:
        h = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
        if np.all(np.isfinite(h)):
            cand = _normalize(_smooth_b_from_h(h), float(n))
            sc = _fast_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < base_score:
                return cand, sc
    return b, base_score


def _evaluate_candidate(a):
    """Call the provided evaluator without importing it."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return np.inf
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if float(np.sum(a)) < 0.01:
        return np.inf
    try:
        sc = float(evaluate_sequence(a.tolist()))
    except Exception:
        return np.inf
    return sc if np.isfinite(sc) else np.inf


def _maybe_update(best_seq, best_score, candidate):
    """Normalize candidate and update if exact evaluator improves score."""
    cand = np.asarray(candidate, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = cand.size
    if n <= 0:
        return best_seq, best_score
    cand = _normalize(cand, float(n))
    sc = _evaluate_candidate(cand)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
        return cand, sc
    return best_seq, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=12345, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Top-level search entry point.

    Runs a coarse global search, quick full-length trials, a long annealed
    natural-gradient refinement, optional L-BFGS polish, length increase,
    and a final small-step polish.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s) - 30.0)
    end_time = start_time + total_budget

    # Load parent if available.
    parent = None
    default_n = 5582
    try:
        parent = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is not None and parent.size > 50 and np.all(np.isfinite(parent)):
        if parent.size > 12000:
            parent = _interpolate(parent, 8000)
        if float(np.sum(np.maximum(parent, 0.0))) > 1e-9:
            parent = _normalize(parent, float(parent.size))
        else:
            parent = None
    else:
        parent = None

    if parent is None:
        n0 = int(default_n)
        parent = _normalize(np.ones(n0), float(n0))

    n0 = int(parent.size)
    best_exact = parent.copy()
    exact_best = _evaluate_candidate(best_exact)
    if not np.isfinite(exact_best):
        exact_best = 2.0
        best_exact = _normalize(np.ones(n0), float(n0))

    print(f"initial parent score = {exact_best:.7f}, n = {n0}")

    # ---------------- Coarse global search ----------------
    coarse_chosen = []
    if end_time - time.monotonic() > 350:
        m = 256
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
        coarse_inits = []
        if parent.size != m:
            coarse_inits.append(("parent_coarse", _interpolate(parent, m)))
        else:
            coarse_inits.append(("parent_coarse", parent.copy()))

        coarse_inits.append(("exp_05", np.exp(0.5 * x)))
        coarse_inits.append(("exp_10", np.exp(1.0 * x)))
        coarse_inits.append(("exp_15", np.exp(1.5 * x)))
        coarse_inits.append(("exp_20", np.exp(2.0 * x)))
        coarse_inits.append(("ramp", 1.0 + 1.5 * x))
        coarse_inits.append(("inv_ramp", 1.0 + 1.5 * (1.0 - x)))
        coarse_inits.append(("rand", 1.0 + 0.5 * rng.random(m)))
        coarse_inits.append(("sine", 1.0 + 0.9 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * x)))

        coarse_budget = min(120.0, max(20.0, total_budget * 0.05))
        deadline = min(end_time - 150, time.monotonic() + coarse_budget)
        per = max(2.0, coarse_budget / max(1, len(coarse_inits)))
        candidates = []

        for ci, (name, init) in enumerate(coarse_inits):
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 2:
                break
            t_end = min(time.monotonic() + per, deadline)
            cb, cs = _nm_search(
                init, t_end, seed=(1000 + int(seed) + ci * 17),
                lr_start=0.18, lr_end=0.03,
                K_start=4, K_end=80, temp_frac=0.015, beta=0.88
            )
            if np.isfinite(cs):
                candidates.append((float(cs), name, cb))
                print(f"coarse {name}: {cs:.7f}")

        candidates.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
        # Keep at most two good coarse shapes.
        for sc, name, arr in candidates[:2]:
            if sc < 1.75 or len(candidates) <= 2:
                coarse_chosen.append((name, np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)))

    # ---------------- Full-length quick trials ----------------
    full_inits = [("exact_best", best_exact.copy())]
    for cname, carr in coarse_chosen[:2]:
        full_inits.append((cname + "_full", _interpolate(carr, n0)))

    xf = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n0)
    full_inits.append(("exp_full", np.exp(1.0 * xf)))
    full_inits.append(("ramp_full", 1.0 + 1.5 * xf))
    full_inits.append(("noise_full", _perturb_sequence(best_exact, rng, 0.12)))
    full_inits = full_inits[:6]

    quick_budget = min(240.0, max(40.0, total_budget * 0.10))
    if end_time - time.monotonic() > quick_budget + 500:
        deadline = min(end_time - 500, time.monotonic() + quick_budget)
        per = max(15.0, quick_budget / max(1, len(full_inits)))
        for ii, (name, init) in enumerate(full_inits):
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 5:
                break
            t_end = min(time.monotonic() + per, deadline)
            cand, score = _nm_search(
                init, t_end, seed=(2000 + int(seed) + ii * 29),
                lr_start=0.08, lr_end=0.02,
                K_start=16, K_end=128, temp_frac=0.010, beta=0.90
            )
            if np.isfinite(score):
                print(f"quick {name}: internal {score:.7f}")
                if time.monotonic() < end_time - 60:
                    best_exact, exact_best = _maybe_update(best_exact, exact_best, cand)

    # ---------------- Main long refinement ----------------
    main_budget = min(1200.0, max(120.0, total_budget * 0.45))
    if end_time - time.monotonic() > main_budget * 0.25:
        deadline = min(end_time - 350, time.monotonic() + main_budget)
        cand, score = _nm_search(
            best_exact, deadline, seed=(3000 + int(seed)),
            lr_start=0.075, lr_end=0.008,
            K_start=16, K_end=240, temp_frac=0.008, beta=0.91
        )
        if np.isfinite(score):
            print(f"main: internal {score:.7f}")
        if time.monotonic() < end_time - 90:
            best_exact, exact_best = _maybe_update(best_exact, exact_best, cand)

    # ---------------- L-BFGS polish ----------------
    if end_time - time.monotonic() > 150 and minimize is not None:
        cand, score = _lbfgs_refine(best_exact, max_iter=25, T=8.0)
        if np.isfinite(score):
            print(f"lbfgs: internal {score:.7f}")
        if time.monotonic() < end_time - 90:
            best_exact, exact_best = _maybe_update(best_exact, exact_best, cand)

    # ---------------- Alternative long run / length increase ----------------
    if end_time - time.monotonic() > 500 and exact_best > 1.5020:
        # Try length increase if reasonable.
        n2_candidate = int(max(n0 + 200, 1.22 * n0))
        n2 = min(8192, n2_candidate)
        if n2 > n0:
            init2 = _interpolate(best_exact, n2)
            budget2 = min(350.0, max(60.0, total_budget * 0.13))
            deadline2 = min(end_time - 250, time.monotonic() + budget2)
            cand2, score2 = _nm_search(
                init2, deadline2, seed=(4000 + int(seed)),
                lr_start=0.07, lr_end=0.010,
                K_start=16, K_end=220, temp_frac=0.008, beta=0.90
            )
            if np.isfinite(score2):
                print(f"length increase n={n2}: internal {score2:.7f}")
            if time.monotonic() < end_time - 90:
                best_exact, exact_best = _maybe_update(best_exact, exact_best, cand2)
        else:
            # Otherwise do a second asymmetric local search.
            init2 = _perturb_sequence(best_exact, rng, 0.18)
            budget2 = min(300.0, max(60.0, total_budget * 0.10))
            deadline2 = min(end_time - 250, time.monotonic() + budget2)
            cand2, score2 = _nm_search(
                init2, deadline2, seed=(4500 + int(seed)),
                lr_start=0.065, lr_end=0.012,
                K_start=32, K_end=200, temp_frac=0.012, beta=0.88
            )
            if np.isfinite(score2):
                print(f"alt long: internal {score2:.7f}")
            if time.monotonic() < end_time - 90:
                best_exact, exact_best = _maybe_update(best_exact, exact_best, cand2)

    # ---------------- Final fine polish ----------------
    if end_time - time.monotonic() > 60:
        deadline = max(time.monotonic() + 5.0, end_time - 30.0)
        cand, score = _nm_search(
            best_exact, deadline, seed=(5000 + int(seed)),
            lr_start=0.025, lr_end=0.002,
            K_start=64, K_end=360, temp_frac=0.005, beta=0.93,
            max_step=1.0, noise_sigma=0.08
        )
        if np.isfinite(score):
            print(f"polish: internal {score:.7f}")
        if time.monotonic() < end_time - 10:
            best_exact, exact_best = _maybe_update(best_exact, exact_best, cand)

    # ---------------- Finalization ----------------
    best_exact = _normalize(best_exact, float(best_exact.size))
    final_score = _evaluate_candidate(best_exact)
    print(f"final exact score = {final_score:.7f}, n = {best_exact.size}")

    if not np.isfinite(final_score):
        fallback = _normalize(np.ones(n0), float(n0))
        return [float(x) for x in fallback]

    return [float(x) for x in best_exact]
```
